South Africa signed the Rio [Convention on Biological Diversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_on_Biological_Diversity "Convention on Biological Diversity") on 4 June 1994 and became a party to the convention on 2 November 1995.[\[144\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-148) It has subsequently produced a [National Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_action_plan "Biodiversity action plan"), which was received by the convention on 7 June 2006.[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-cbd.int-149) The country is ranked sixth out of the world's seventeen [megadiverse countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadiverse_countries "Megadiverse countries").[\[146\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-150) [Ecotourism in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecotourism_in_South_Africa "Ecotourism in South Africa") has become more prevalent in recent years, as a possible method of maintaining and improving biodiversity.

Numerous mammals are found in the Bushveld including lions, [African leopards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_leopard "African leopard"), [South African cheetahs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_African_cheetah "Southeast African cheetah"), [southern white rhinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_white_rhinoceros "Southern white rhinoceros"), [blue wildebeest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_wildebeest "Blue wildebeest"), [kudus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kudu "Kudu"), [impalas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impala "Impala"), [hyenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyena "Hyena"), [hippopotamuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hippopotamus "Hippopotamus") and [South African giraffes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_giraffe "South African giraffe"). A significant extent of the Bushveld exists in the north-east including Kruger National Park and the [Sabi Sand Game Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabi_Sand_Game_Reserve "Sabi Sand Game Reserve"), as well as in the far north in the [Waterberg Biosphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterberg_Biosphere "Waterberg Biosphere"). South Africa houses many [endemic species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endemism "Endemism"), among them the critically endangered [riverine rabbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riverine_rabbit "Riverine rabbit") ( _Bunolagus monticullaris_) in the Karoo.

Up to 1945, more than 4,900 species of [fungi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fungus "Fungus") (including [lichen-forming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lichen#Fungi "Lichen") species) had been recorded.[\[147\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-151) In 2006, the number of fungi in South Africa was estimated at 200,000 species but did not take into account fungi associated with insects.[\[148\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-152) If correct, then the number of South African fungi dwarfs that of its plants. In at least some major South African ecosystems, an exceptionally high percentage of fungi are highly specific in terms of the plants with which they occur.[\[149\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-153) The country's Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan does not mention fungi (including lichen-forming fungi).[\[145\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-cbd.int-149)

With more than 22,000 different [vascular plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vascular_plant "Vascular plant"), or about 9% of all the known species of plants on Earth,[\[150\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-154) South Africa is particularly rich in plant diversity. The most prevalent biome is the [grassland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grassland "Grassland"), particularly on the Highveld, where the plant cover is dominated by different [grasses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poaceae "Poaceae"), low shrubs, and [acacia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia "Acacia"), mainly camel-thorn ( _[Vachellia erioloba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vachellia_erioloba "Vachellia erioloba")_). Vegetation is sparse towards the north-west because of low rainfall. There are numerous species of water-storing succulents, like [aloes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aloe "Aloe") and [euphorbias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphorbia "Euphorbia"), in the very hot and dry Namaqualand area. And according to the [World Wildlife Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature "World Wide Fund for Nature"), South Africa is home to around a third of all succulent species.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-Trenchard-2021-155) The grass and thorn [savanna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savanna "Savanna") turns slowly into a bush savanna towards the north-east of the country, with denser growth. There are significant numbers of [baobab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adansonia "Adansonia") trees in this area, near the northern end of Kruger National Park.[\[152\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-156)

The [fynbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fynbos "Fynbos") biome, which makes up the majority of the area and plant life in the [Cape Floristic Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Floristic_Region "Cape Floristic Region"), is located in a small region of the Western Cape and contains more than 9,000 of those species, or three times more plant species than found in the [Amazon rainforest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_rainforest "Amazon rainforest"),[\[153\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-157) making it among the richest regions on Earth in terms of plant diversity. Most of the plants are [evergreen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evergreen "Evergreen") hard-leaf plants with fine, needle-like leaves, such as the [sclerophyllous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sclerophyll "Sclerophyll") plants. Another uniquely South African flowering plant group is the genus _[Protea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protea "Protea")_, with around 130 different species. While South Africa has a great wealth of flowering plants, only 1% of the land is forest, almost exclusively in the humid [coastal plain of KwaZulu-Natal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KwaZulu%E2%80%93Cape_coastal_forest_mosaic "KwaZulu–Cape coastal forest mosaic"), where there are also areas of [Southern Africa mangroves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Africa_mangroves "Southern Africa mangroves") in river mouths. Even smaller reserves of forests are out of the reach of fire, known as [montane forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knysna%E2%80%93Amatole_montane_forests "Knysna–Amatole montane forests"). Plantations of imported tree species are predominant, particularly the non-native [eucalyptus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eucalyptus "Eucalyptus") and pine.

South Africa has lost a large area of natural habitat in the last four decades, primarily because of overpopulation, sprawling development patterns, and deforestation during the 19th century. The country had a 2019 [Forest Landscape Integrity Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index "Forest Landscape Integrity Index") mean score of 4.94/10, ranking it 112th globally out of 172 countries.[\[154\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-158) South Africa is one of the worst affected countries in the world when it comes to invasion by [alien species](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Introduced_species "Introduced species") with many (e.g., [black wattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia_mearnsii "Acacia mearnsii"), [Port Jackson willow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acacia_saligna "Acacia saligna"), _[Hakea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakea "Hakea")_, _[Lantana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lantana "Lantana")_ and _[Jacaranda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacaranda "Jacaranda")_) posing a significant threat to the native biodiversity and the already scarce water resources. Also, [woody plant encroachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woody_plant_encroachment "Woody plant encroachment") of native plants in grasslands poses a threat to biodiversity and related ecosystem services, affecting over 7 million hectares.[\[155\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-159) The original [temperate forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temperate_broadleaf_and_mixed_forests "Temperate broadleaf and mixed forests") found by the first European settlers was exploited until only small patches remained. Currently, South African hardwood trees like [real yellowwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_yellowwood "Real yellowwood") ( _[Podocarpus latifolius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podocarpus_latifolius "Podocarpus latifolius")_), stinkwood ( _[Ocotea bullata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocotea_bullata "Ocotea bullata")_), and South African black ironwood ( _[Olea capensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olea_capensis "Olea capensis")_) are under strict government protection. Statistics from the [Department of Environmental Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Environment,_Forestry_and_Fisheries "Department of Environment, Forestry and Fisheries") show a record 1,215 rhinos were killed in 2014.[\[156\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-160) Since South Africa is home to a third of all succulent species (many endemic to the Karoo), it makes it a hotspot for plant poaching, leading to many species to be threatened with extinction.[\[151\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-Trenchard-2021-155)

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Government of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_South_Africa "Government of South Africa"), [Politics of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_South_Africa "Politics of South Africa"), [Elections in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_South_Africa "Elections in South Africa"), and [Law of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_South_Africa "Law of South Africa")

See also: [Human rights in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_South_Africa "Human rights in South Africa") and [LGBT rights in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_South_Africa "LGBT rights in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/President_of_South_Africa_Cyril_Ramaphosa_%283x4_cropped%29.jpg/330px-President_of_South_Africa_Cyril_Ramaphosa_%283x4_cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:President_of_South_Africa_Cyril_Ramaphosa_(3x4_cropped).jpg)

[Cyril Ramaphosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyril_Ramaphosa "Cyril Ramaphosa")

[President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_South_Africa "President of South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/David_Lammy_with_Paul_Mashatile_%28cropped%29.jpg/330px-David_Lammy_with_Paul_Mashatile_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:David_Lammy_with_Paul_Mashatile_(cropped).jpg)

[Paul Mashatile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Mashatile "Paul Mashatile")

[Deputy President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_President_of_South_Africa "Deputy President of South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Uniegebou.jpg/500px-Uniegebou.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Uniegebou.jpg) [Union Buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Buildings "Union Buildings") in Pretoria, seat of the executive[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Parliament_in_company_gardens_%28cropped%29.jpg/500px-Parliament_in_company_gardens_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Parliament_in_company_gardens_(cropped).jpg) Houses of [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_South_Africa "Parliament of South Africa") in Cape Town, seat of the legislature[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Constitutional_Court_of_South_Africa.jpg/500px-Constitutional_Court_of_South_Africa.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Constitutional_Court_of_South_Africa.jpg) [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_South_Africa "Constitutional Court of South Africa") in Johannesburg

South Africa is a [parliamentary republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_republic "Parliamentary republic"), but unlike most such republics, the [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_South_Africa "President of South Africa") is both [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state "Head of state") and [head of government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_government "Head of government") and depends for their tenure on the [confidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confidence_and_supply "Confidence and supply") of [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_South_Africa "Parliament of South Africa"). The executive, legislature, and judiciary are all subject to the supremacy of the [Constitution of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_South_Africa "Constitution of South Africa"), and the [superior courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courts_of_South_Africa "Courts of South Africa") have the power to strike down executive actions and acts of Parliament if they are unconstitutional. The [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_South_Africa "National Assembly of South Africa"), the lower house of Parliament, consists of 400 members and is elected every five years by a system of [party-list proportional representation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party-list_proportional_representation "Party-list proportional representation"). The [National Council of Provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Council_of_Provinces "National Council of Provinces"), the upper house, consists of ninety members, with each of the nine [provincial legislatures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_legislature_(South_Africa) "Provincial legislature (South Africa)") electing ten members.

After each parliamentary election, the National Assembly elects one of its members as president; hence the president serves a term of office the same as that of the Assembly, normally five years. No president may serve more than two terms in office.[\[157\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-161) The president appoints a [deputy president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_President_of_South_Africa "Deputy President of South Africa") and [ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_(government) "Minister (government)") (each representing a [department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_(government_department) "Ministry (government department)")) who form the [cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_South_Africa "Cabinet of South Africa"). The National Assembly may remove the president and the cabinet by a [motion of no confidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motion_of_no_confidence "Motion of no confidence"). In the [most recent election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_South_African_general_election "2024 South African general election"), held on 29 May 2024, the ANC lost its majority for the first time since the end of Apartheid,[\[158\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-162) winning only 40% of the vote and 159 seats, while the main opposition, the [Democratic Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Alliance_(South_Africa) "Democratic Alliance (South Africa)") (DA), won 22% of the vote and 87 seats. [uMkhonto weSizwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UMkhonto_weSizwe_(political_party) "UMkhonto weSizwe (political party)"), a new party founded by former [President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_South_Africa "President of South Africa") and ANC leader [Jacob Zuma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Zuma "Jacob Zuma"), won 14.6% of the vote and 58 seats, while the [Economic Freedom Fighters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_Freedom_Fighters "Economic Freedom Fighters"), founded by [Julius Malema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Malema "Julius Malema"), former president of the [ANC Youth League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_National_Congress_Youth_League "African National Congress Youth League") who was later expelled from the ANC, won 9.5% of the vote and 39 seats. After the election, the ANC formed a [Government of National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_National_Unity_(South_Africa) "Government of National Unity (South Africa)") with the DA and several smaller parties.[\[159\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-163)

South Africa has no legally defined capital city. The fourth chapter of the constitution states "The seat of Parliament is Cape Town, but an Act of Parliament enacted in accordance with section 76(1) and (5) may determine that the seat of Parliament is elsewhere."[\[160\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-164) The country's three branches of government are split over different cities. Cape Town, as the seat of Parliament, is the legislative capital; Pretoria, as the seat of the president and cabinet, is the administrative capital; and Bloemfontein is the seat of the [Supreme Court of Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Appeal_(South_Africa) "Supreme Court of Appeal (South Africa)"), and is regarded as the judicial capital;[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-Marais_Twala_2020_pp._49%E2%80%9362-24) although the highest court, the [Constitutional Court of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_South_Africa "Constitutional Court of South Africa") has been based in Johannesburg since 1994. Most foreign embassies are located in Pretoria.

Since 2004, South Africa has had many thousands of popular protests,[\[161\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-165) some violent, making it, according to one academic, the "most protest-rich country in the world".[\[162\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-166) There have been numerous incidents of [political repression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_repression_in_post-apartheid_South_Africa "Political repression in post-apartheid South Africa") as well as threats of future repression in violation of the constitution, leading some analysts and [civil society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_society "Civil society") organisations to conclude that there is or could be a new climate of political repression.[\[163\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-167)[\[164\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-168)

In 2022, South Africa was placed sixth out of 48 sub-Saharan African countries on the [Ibrahim Index of African Governance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_Index_of_African_Governance "Ibrahim Index of African Governance"). South Africa scored well in the categories of [Rule of Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rule_of_law "Rule of law"), [Transparency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transparency_(behavior) "Transparency (behavior)"), Corruption, [Participation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_participation_(decision_making) "Public participation (decision making)") and Human Rights, but scored low in Safety and Security.[\[165\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-169) In 2006, South Africa became one of the first jurisdictions in the world to legalise [same-sex marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_South_Africa "Same-sex marriage in South Africa").[\[166\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-170)[\[167\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-NYTimes-171)

The Constitution of South Africa is the supreme rule of law in the country. The primary sources of [South African law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_South_Africa "Law of South Africa") are [Roman-Dutch mercantile law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman-Dutch_law "Roman-Dutch law") and personal law and [English Common law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_law "English law"), as imports of Dutch settlements and British colonialism.[\[168\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-172) The first European-based law in South Africa was brought by the Dutch East India Company and is called Roman-Dutch law. It was imported before the [codification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Codification_(law) "Codification (law)") of European law into the [Napoleonic Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonic_Code "Napoleonic Code") and is comparable in many ways to [Scots law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scots_law "Scots law"). This was followed in the 19th century by English law, both [common](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_law "Common law") and [statutory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutory_law "Statutory law"). After unification in 1910, South Africa had its own parliament which passed laws specific for South Africa, building on those previously passed for the individual member colonies. The judicial system consists of the [magistrates' courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magistrate's_court_(South_Africa) "Magistrate's court (South Africa)"), which hear lesser criminal cases and smaller civil cases; the [High Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Court_of_South_Africa "High Court of South Africa"), which has divisions that serve as the courts of [general jurisdiction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_jurisdiction "General jurisdiction") for specific areas; the Supreme Court of Appeal; and the Constitutional Court, which is the highest court.

### Administrative divisions

Main articles: [Administrative divisions of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_South_Africa "Administrative divisions of South Africa") and [Provinces of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_South_Africa "Provinces of South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Map_of_South_Africa_with_English_labels.svg/960px-Map_of_South_Africa_with_English_labels.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Map_of_South_Africa_with_English_labels.svg) [Provinces of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_of_South_Africa "Provinces of South Africa")

Each of the nine provinces is governed by a [unicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unicameralism "Unicameralism") [legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_legislature_(South_Africa) "Provincial legislature (South Africa)"), which is elected every five years by [party-list proportional representation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party-list_proportional_representation "Party-list proportional representation"). The legislature elects a [premier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_(South_Africa) "Premier (South Africa)") as head of government, and the premier appoints an [Executive Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Council_(South_Africa) "Executive Council (South Africa)") as a provincial cabinet. The powers of provincial governments are limited to topics listed in the constitution; these topics include such fields as health, education, public housing and transport.

The provinces are in turn divided into 52 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_South_Africa "Districts of South Africa"): eight [metropolitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipality_(South_Africa) "Metropolitan municipality (South Africa)") and 44 [district municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_municipality_(South_Africa) "District municipality (South Africa)"). The district municipalities are further subdivided into 205 [local municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_municipality_(South_Africa) "Local municipality (South Africa)"). The metropolitan municipalities, which govern the largest urban agglomerations, perform the functions of both district and local municipalities.
