### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_South_Africa "Foreign relations of South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/PM_in_a_family_photograph_during_the_BRICS_Leaders_Retreat_Meeting%2C_at_Johannesburg%2C_in_South_Africa_on_August_23%2C_2023_%281%29.jpg/500px-PM_in_a_family_photograph_during_the_BRICS_Leaders_Retreat_Meeting%2C_at_Johannesburg%2C_in_South_Africa_on_August_23%2C_2023_%281%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:PM_in_a_family_photograph_during_the_BRICS_Leaders_Retreat_Meeting,_at_Johannesburg,_in_South_Africa_on_August_23,_2023_(1).jpg) Ramaphosa and other [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS "BRICS") leaders during the [15th BRICS Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/15th_BRICS_summit "15th BRICS summit") in Johannesburg, August 2023

As the Union of South Africa, the country is a founding member of the [United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations "United Nations") (UN), with Prime Minister [Jan Smuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Smuts "Jan Smuts") writing the [preamble to the UN Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preamble_to_the_United_Nations_Charter "Preamble to the United Nations Charter").[\[170\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-gildersleeve-174)[\[171\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-Schlesinger-175) South Africa is one of the founding members of the [African Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Union "African Union") (AU) and has the [largest economy of all the members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_African_countries_by_GDP_(nominal) "List of African countries by GDP (nominal)"). It is a founding member of the AU's [New Partnership for Africa's Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Partnership_for_Africa's_Development "New Partnership for Africa's Development"). After apartheid ended, South Africa was readmitted to the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations "Commonwealth of Nations"). The country is a member of the [Group of 77](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_77 "Group of 77") and chaired the organisation in 2006. South Africa is also a member of the [Southern African Development Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_African_Development_Community "Southern African Development Community"), [South Atlantic Peace and Cooperation Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Atlantic_Peace_and_Cooperation_Zone "South Atlantic Peace and Cooperation Zone"), [Southern African Customs Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_African_Customs_Union "Southern African Customs Union"), [Antarctic Treaty System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antarctic_Treaty_System "Antarctic Treaty System"), [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization "World Trade Organization"), International Monetary Fund, [G20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G20 "G20"), [G8+5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G8+5 "G8+5"), and the [Port Management Association of Eastern and Southern Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Management_Association_of_Eastern_and_Southern_Africa "Port Management Association of Eastern and Southern Africa").

South Africa has played a key role as a mediator in African conflicts over the last decade, such as in Burundi, the Democratic Republic of the Congo, Comoros, Mozambique and Zimbabwe.[\[172\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-176)

President [Jacob Zuma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Zuma "Jacob Zuma") and Chinese President [Hu Jintao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hu_Jintao "Hu Jintao") upgraded bilateral ties between the two countries in 2010 when they signed the Beijing Agreement which elevated South Africa's earlier "strategic partnership" with China to the higher level of "comprehensive strategic partnership" in both economic and political affairs, including the strengthening of exchanges between their respective ruling parties and legislatures.[\[173\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-177)[\[174\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-178) In 2011, South Africa joined the Brazil-Russia-India-China ( [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS "BRICS")) grouping of countries, identified by Zuma as the country's largest trading partners and also the largest trading partners with Africa as a whole. Zuma asserted that BRICS member countries would also work with each other through the UN, G20, and the India, Brazil South Africa ( [IBSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBSA_Dialogue_Forum "IBSA Dialogue Forum")) forum.[\[175\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-179)

### Military

Main article: [South African National Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Defence_Force "South African National Defence Force")

Further information: [Defence industry of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_industry_of_South_Africa "Defence industry of South Africa") and [South Africa and weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction "South Africa and weapons of mass destruction")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Swartkops_Airshow-152.jpg/500px-Swartkops_Airshow-152.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Swartkops_Airshow-152.jpg) [South African Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Army "South African Army") soldiers on manoeuvre with a locally developed [Denel Rooivalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denel_Rooivalk "Denel Rooivalk") attack helicopter operated by the [South African Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Air_Force "South African Air Force")

The [South African National Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Defence_Force "South African National Defence Force") (SANDF) serves as the unified [armed forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military "Military") of South Africa. Established in 1994,[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-constitution-1993-224-180) it was formed as a [voluntary military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volunteer_military "Volunteer military") through the integration of the former [South African Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Defence_Force "South African Defence Force") (SADF) and several [liberation movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_movement "Liberation movement") forces.[\[176\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-constitution-1993-224-180) The SANDF is organised into four branches: the [South African Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Army "South African Army"), the [South African Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Air_Force "South African Air Force"), the [South African Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Navy "South African Navy"), and the [South African Military Health Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Military_Health_Service "South African Military Health Service"). As of 2025, the force comprises nearly 100,000 personnel, of whom around 75,000 are active [professional soldiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_army "Standing army"), and operates under the authority of the president of South Africa, who serves as [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief "Commander-in-chief").[\[177\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-act-42-2002-181) The SANDF emerged as a prominent African [peacekeeping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peacekeeping "Peacekeeping") force in the early 2000s,[\[178\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-182) deploying as a leading contributor to United Nations and African Union missions in [Burundi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burundian_Civil_War "Burundian Civil War") (2001), [Sudan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Darfur "War in Darfur") (2004) and the [Democratic Republic of the Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MONUSCO "MONUSCO") (2003). It has since participated in regional security operations in countries including [Lesotho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_intervention_in_Lesotho "South African intervention in Lesotho") and [Mozambique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurgency_in_Cabo_Delgado "Insurgency in Cabo Delgado"), while continuing to contribute to multinational [United Nations peacekeeping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_peacekeeping "United Nations peacekeeping") missions, including the [Force Intervention Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Force_Intervention_Brigade "United Nations Force Intervention Brigade").[\[179\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-dod-sep2005-183)

Supporting these armed forces is a [domestic defence industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_industry_of_South_Africa "Defence industry of South Africa") that is the most advanced in Africa and one of the world's leading [defence industrial bases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industrial_base "Defense industrial base") outside the major military powers.[\[180\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-184) Coordinated by the state-owned [Armaments Corporation of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armscor_(South_Africa) "Armscor (South Africa)") (Armscor), it includes major companies such as [Denel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denel "Denel"), [Paramount Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Group "Paramount Group"), and [Milkor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milkor "Milkor"). The industry produces a wide range of advanced systems, including armoured vehicles, military aircraft, naval vessels, and missiles, with about 80% of SANDF equipment made in South Africa.[\[181\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-weapons2-185) The country also remains a significant arms exporter and maintains self-sufficiency in most conventional military technologies.[\[182\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-186)

South Africa is the only African nation to have successfully developed [nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapon "Nuclear weapon").[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-fas-ocp2722-187) Between 1980 and 1990, six operational nuclear devices were covertly assembled before the arsenal was voluntarily dismantled in 1991, making South Africa the first country to relinquish its nuclear capability.[\[183\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-fas-ocp2722-187) The country is also alleged to have conducted a [nuclear test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_testing "Nuclear weapons testing") over the Atlantic in 1979, known as the " [Vela incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vela_incident "Vela incident")", although this is officially denied; then-President F.W. de Klerk later asserted that South Africa had "never conducted a clandestine nuclear test".[\[184\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-188)[\[185\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-Sclean2-189) Despite dismantling its arsenal, South Africa still has the technical capability to restart its [weapons of mass destruction programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction "South Africa and weapons of mass destruction") if it ever chose to do so, as its [Pelindaba Nuclear Research Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelindaba "Pelindaba") still stores [enriched uranium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enriched_uranium "Enriched uranium") from the former programme.[\[186\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-190) However, this material is now used primarily for civilian nuclear research and [medical isotope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_isotope "Medical isotope") production, reflecting South Africa's commitment to the peaceful use of nuclear energy.[\[187\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-191)[\[188\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-192) South Africa remains a strong advocate of [nuclear disarmament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_disarmament "Nuclear disarmament"), having ratified the UN [Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons "Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons") in 2019.[\[189\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-193)[\[190\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-194)

### Law enforcement and crime

Main articles: [Law enforcement in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_South_Africa "Law enforcement in South Africa") and [Crime in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_South_Africa "Crime in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/South_african_police_may_2010.png/500px-South_african_police_may_2010.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:South_african_police_may_2010.png) Officers of the [South African Police Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Police_Service "South African Police Service") with [Vektor R5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R4_assault_rifle "R4 assault rifle") rifles on parade in Johannesburg, 2010

Law enforcement in South Africa is primarily the responsibility of the [South African Police Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Police_Service "South African Police Service") (SAPS), the national police force operating more than 1,150 police stations and employing around 150,950 officers.[\[191\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-195) The SAPS is tasked with crime prevention, investigation, and national security. It also maintains an elite tactical unit, the [Special Task Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Task_Force_(SAPS) "Special Task Force (SAPS)") (STF), which specialises in [counter-terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterterrorism "Counterterrorism"), [counter-insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterinsurgency "Counterinsurgency"), and [hostage rescue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hostage_rescue "Hostage rescue") operations. In the 2023 International SWAT Competition, the STF ranked 9th out of 55 international law enforcement teams, making it the highest-ranked African police unit and one of the world's best.[\[192\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-196) Alongside formal policing, [South Africa has the world's largest private security industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_security_industry_in_South_Africa "Private security industry in South Africa"),[\[193\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-197) comprising over 10,000 companies and more than 2.5 million registered personnel,[\[194\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-198) exceeding the combined size of both the police and the military.[\[195\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-199)[\[196\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-200)

South Africa continues to experience high levels of [violent crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violent_crime_in_South_Africa "Violent crime in South Africa"), among the highest in the world and it leads Africa in this regard.[\[197\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-201) From April 2017 to March 2018, an average of 57 murders per day were recorded, with a [homicide rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_intentional_homicide_rate "List of countries by intentional homicide rate") more than five times the global average.[\[198\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-202) Serious crimes such as armed robbery, hijackings, [cash-in-transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cash-in-transit "Cash-in-transit") heists, gang-related killings, and [sexual violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_violence_in_South_Africa "Sexual violence in South Africa") are common.[\[199\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-203)[\[200\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-204) South Africa also records one of the world's highest [reported rates of rape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rape_statistics "Rape statistics"),[\[201\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-205) with tens of thousands of cases each year, though many go unreported.[\[202\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-RapeStats2-206) Between 1994 and 2019, more than 526,000 murders were reported nationwide.[\[203\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-207) [Gang violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gangs_in_South_Africa "Gangs in South Africa") remains a major driver of the homicide rate, particularly in the [Cape Flats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Flats "Cape Flats") region of Cape Town, where rival groups compete over territory, [drug trafficking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_trafficking "Drug trafficking") routes, and extortion.[\[204\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-208) These conflicts often result in civilian casualties, with bystanders and children frequently caught in the crossfire.[\[205\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-209)

Despite ongoing reforms, South Africa's criminal justice system faces persistent challenges, including corruption, inefficiency, incompetency, and underreporting, which have undermined public confidence in law enforcement. The [Department of Justice and Constitutional Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Justice_and_Constitutional_Development "Department of Justice and Constitutional Development") oversees the court system, while the [Department of Correctional Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Correctional_Services_(South_Africa) "Department of Correctional Services (South Africa)") manages the country's prisons.[\[206\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-210) South Africa has the highest prison population in Africa,[\[207\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-211) with chronic overcrowding and human rights concerns frequently reported.[\[208\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-212) Critics argue that systemic weaknesses and uneven access to justice have fostered a culture of impunity, sustaining high crime rates and public mistrust. These issues continue to shape national debates on security, governance, and human rights.[\[209\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-213)

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_South_Africa "Economy of South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Johannesburg_Skyline.jpg/500px-Johannesburg_Skyline.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Johannesburg_Skyline.jpg) [Johannesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johannesburg "Johannesburg"), the economic centre of South Africa and the continent's leading [financial centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre "Financial centre")[\[210\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-214)

South Africa has a [mixed economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_economy "Mixed economy") characterised by a large [services sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_sector "Service sector"), diversified industrial base and abundant [natural resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_resource "Natural resource"). It is the [largest economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_African_countries_by_GDP_(nominal) "List of African countries by GDP (nominal)") in Africa and is widely recognised as the continent's most industrialised, technologically advanced and diversified nation.[\[211\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-215)[\[212\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-216)[\[213\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-217) As of 2026, South Africa has a nominal [gross domestic product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_domestic_product "Gross domestic product") (GDP) exceeding US$450 billion, making it the world's [39th-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal) "List of countries by GDP (nominal)") economy and one of the highest [GDPs per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita "List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita") in sub-Saharan Africa. The [South African rand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_rand "South African rand") (ZAR) is Africa's most traded currency,[\[214\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-218) while the [Johannesburg Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JSE_Limited "JSE Limited") (JSE) is the continent's largest [stock exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_exchange "Stock exchange") and ranks among the world's 20 largest by [market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_capitalization "Market capitalization").[\[215\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:12-219) South Africa is the only African member of the [G20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G20 "G20") and is also a member of [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS "BRICS").[\[216\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-220)

In 2023, South Africa ranked 106th on the [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index "Human Development Index") (HDI), placing it in the high human development category.[\[217\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-2022_components3-221) Its [labour force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_force "Labour force") numbered approximately 27.8 million in 2024, the [26th-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_labour_force "List of countries by labour force") in the world.[\[218\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-222) Despite its economic development, South Africa continues to face high [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment "Unemployment"), widespread [poverty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty "Poverty") and severe [income inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inequality_in_post-apartheid_South_Africa "Inequality in post-apartheid South Africa"),[\[219\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-223)[\[220\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-worldbank-poverty2-224) with an official unemployment rate of 33.6% as of the second quarter of 2026.[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:0-225) Approximately 23 million people (37.9% of the population) live below the [lower-bound poverty line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_threshold "Poverty threshold"),[\[222\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-226) while 10.8 million live below the [food poverty line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_South_Africa "Poverty in South Africa"),[\[221\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:0-225) and the country consistently records one of the world's highest [Gini coefficients](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gini_coefficient "Gini coefficient").[\[223\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-227)[\[224\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-228)[\[225\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-229)

South Africa is a major [trading nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trading_nation "Trading nation"), ranking among the world's 50 largest exporters and importers. Its principal exports include minerals, precious metals, vehicles, machinery, agricultural products and manufactured goods.[\[226\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-factbook2222-230) As of 2026, its largest export markets are [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China "China") (12.5%), the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States") (7.7%), [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany "Germany") (7.4%), [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan "Japan") (5.6%) and the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom "United Kingdom") (4.0%), while its principal import sources are China (22.7%), [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India "India") (12.2%), [Nigeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nigeria "Nigeria") (6.8%), the United States (6.3%) and Germany (5.7%).[\[227\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-231) In 2025, exports accounted for approximately 31.4% of GDP.[\[228\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-232)

### Industries and services

Further information: [Automotive industry in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_South_Africa "Automotive industry in South Africa"), [State-owned enterprises of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-owned_enterprises_of_South_Africa "State-owned enterprises of South Africa"), [Banking in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_in_South_Africa "Banking in South Africa"), and [Retailing in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retailing_in_South_Africa "Retailing in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/BMW_iX1_EV_in_Constantia%2C_Cape_Town_%28cropped%29.jpg/500px-BMW_iX1_EV_in_Constantia%2C_Cape_Town_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:BMW_iX1_EV_in_Constantia,_Cape_Town_(cropped).jpg) A [BMW iX1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_X1_(U11) "BMW X1 (U11)") charging at a [charging station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charging_station "Charging station") in [Constantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantia,_Cape_Town "Constantia, Cape Town"), Cape Town

South Africa is a highly industrialised country with Africa's most diversified industrial sector.[\[229\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-233) Industry accounted for approximately 24% of GDP in 2024, with [manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing_in_South_Africa "Manufacturing in South Africa") contributing around 13% and ranking [40th globally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing#List_of_countries_by_manufacturing_output "Manufacturing") by output.[\[230\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-econ-jobless-234) Major industries include [construction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction "Construction"), motor vehicles, [chemicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substance_(chemistry) "Substance (chemistry)"), [steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel "Steel"), machinery, [processed foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_processing "Food processing"), textiles and military hardware. The country also has [shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipbuilding "Shipbuilding") and [aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace "Aerospace") industries, with a significant focus on defence-related products.[\[231\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-235) [State-owned enterprises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State-owned_enterprise "State-owned enterprise"), including [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom "Eskom"), [Transnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnet "Transnet") and [Denel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denel "Denel"), also play a role in the industrial economy, particularly in the energy, transport and defence sectors.

Within manufacturing, the [automotive industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_South_Africa "Automotive industry in South Africa") is a major component. [Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Motor_Company "Ford Motor Company") opened the country's first assembly plant in 1924, followed by [General Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors "General Motors") in 1926.[\[232\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-236) Other international manufacturers subsequently established assembly operations in the country, including [Toyota](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota "Toyota"), [Volkswagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen "Volkswagen"), [BMW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW "BMW"), [Mercedes-Benz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz "Mercedes-Benz") and [Nissan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nissan "Nissan"). South Africa is Africa's largest [motor vehicle producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_motor_vehicle_production "List of countries by motor vehicle production"), producing around 600,000 vehicles a year,[\[233\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-237) with exports accounting for approximately two-thirds of local production.[\[234\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-238)

[Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector "Tertiary sector") accounted for 63.1% of South Africa's GDP in 2025 and include a well-developed [financial services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_services_in_South_Africa "Financial services in South Africa") industry.[\[235\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-239) Johannesburg is Africa's leading financial centre and home to the JSE,[\[236\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-240) while South Africa's [stock market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_stock_market_capitalization "List of countries by stock market capitalization") reached approximately US$1.53 trillion in 2026, ranking 16th globally.[\[237\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-241) Retail is also significant, with the country having the world's sixth-largest number of [shopping centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shopping_center "Shopping center").[\[238\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-242) Other major services include [real estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_estate "Real estate"), [telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications "Telecommunications") and [business services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporate_services "Corporate services"), with Cape Town serving as a leading [technology hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tech_hub "Tech hub") on the African continent.[\[239\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-243)

South African companies feature prominently among Africa's most valuable businesses, including [AngloGold Ashanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AngloGold_Ashanti "AngloGold Ashanti"), [Gold Fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_Fields "Gold Fields"), [Impala Platinum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impala_Platinum "Impala Platinum"), [Naspers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naspers "Naspers"), [MTN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTN_Group "MTN Group"), [Vodacom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodacom "Vodacom"), [Sanlam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanlam "Sanlam"), [Sasol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasol "Sasol"), [FirstRand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FirstRand "FirstRand") and [Standard Bank Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Bank_Group "Standard Bank Group").[\[240\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-244) Their activities span mining, finance, telecommunications, insurance, chemicals, media and technology, with many operating across Africa and internationally. In a 2026 _[African Business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Business "African Business")_ ranking, all 20 South African companies listed were among the 30 leading African companies by market value.[\[241\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-245)

### Mining

Main article: [Mining in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_in_South_Africa "Mining in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/Platinum_Mining.jpg/500px-Platinum_Mining.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Platinum_Mining.jpg) The [Two Rivers mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Rivers_mine "Two Rivers mine"), jointly owned by [African Rainbow Minerals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Rainbow_Minerals "African Rainbow Minerals") and [Impala Platinum Holdings Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impala_Platinum "Impala Platinum")

The [mining industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry_of_South_Africa "Mining industry of South Africa") remains an important part of South Africa's economy, contributing around 6.0% of GDP in 2024, making South Africa the world's fifth-largest mining economy by contribution to GDP.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:6-246)[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:1-247) Mineral products accounted for around 52% of the value of the country's total exports in 2025.[\[244\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-248) The industry has historically played a major role in the country's industrial development, following the discovery of [diamonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond "Diamond") near the [Orange River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_River "Orange River") in 1867 and [gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold "Gold") on the [Witwatersrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witwatersrand "Witwatersrand") in 1886. The sector is regulated by the [Department of Mineral and Petroleum Resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Mineral_and_Petroleum_Resources "Department of Mineral and Petroleum Resources"), which is responsible for the mining industry, the exploitation of mineral resources and the petroleum industry.[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:6-246)[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:1-247)

South Africa is one of the world's leading producers of mineral resources. In 2024, South Africa accounted for substantial shares of global mined mineral production, including [platinum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platinum "Platinum") (70%), [chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium "Chromium") (46%), [manganese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manganese "Manganese") (40%), [palladium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palladium "Palladium") (38%), [vermiculite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vermiculite "Vermiculite") (35%), [rutile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutile "Rutile") and [zircon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zircon "Zircon") (22% each), [ilmenite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilmenite "Ilmenite") (14%), industrial diamond (8%), [vanadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanadium "Vanadium") (7%), industrial garnet and [talc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talc "Talc") (about 5% each), [fluorspar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fluorite "Fluorite") (4%), and gemstone diamond, gold and [iron ore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_ore "Iron ore") (3% each).[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:6-246)[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:1-247) South Africa also possesses mineral reserves valued at more than US$2.5 trillion,[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:6-246) including substantial shares of global reserves of [platinum-group metals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platinum_group_metals "Platinum group metals") (88%), manganese (80%), [chromite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromite "Chromite") (72%), vanadium (32%), [zirconium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zirconium "Zirconium") (25%), fluorspar (17%), gold (13%), [titanium minerals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Titanium "Titanium") (10%), [uranium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uranium "Uranium") (5%), and [antimony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antimony "Antimony") (2%).[\[242\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:6-246)[\[243\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:1-247)

### Agriculture

Main article: [Agriculture in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_South_Africa "Agriculture in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/RSA_Sugar_Fields_%28cropped%29.jpg/500px-RSA_Sugar_Fields_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:RSA_Sugar_Fields_(cropped).jpg) [Sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane "Sugarcane") fields along the east coast north of Durban

The [agricultural sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_South_Africa "Agriculture in South Africa") contributed approximately 2.7% to South Africa's GDP in 2025 while supporting [food security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_security "Food security"), rural livelihoods and exports.[\[245\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-249) It is considered the most modern, productive and diverse agricultural sector in Africa.[\[246\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-250) According to the 2017 Census of Commercial Agriculture, South Africa had 40,122 commercial farming units, of which 33.9% were engaged in [livestock farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livestock "Livestock"), 31.1% in [mixed farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_farming "Mixed farming") and 21.3% in [field crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crop "Crop"). In September 2018, commercial agriculture covered 46.4 million hectares (114.7 million acres), or 37.9% of the country's land area. Of this land, 36.5 million hectares (78.7%) were used for [grazing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grazing "Grazing") and 7.6 million hectares (16.4%) for [arable farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arable_land "Arable land"), with the remainder devoted to other uses.[\[247\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-251)

South Africa is a leading producer and exporter of [citrus fruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citrus "Citrus"), [grapes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grape "Grape"), [wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wine "Wine"), [apples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple "Apple"), [pears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pear "Pear"), [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize "Maize"), [sugarcane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugarcane "Sugarcane"), [nuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nut_(fruit) "Nut (fruit)") and [wool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wool "Wool").[\[248\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-252) South Africa became the world's largest citrus exporter by volume in 2025, overtaking Spain after exporting approximately 2.9 million tonnes.[\[249\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-253) Its [wine industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_wine "South African wine") is the seventh-largest in the world.[\[250\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-254) South Africa is a net exporter of agricultural products and exports approximately half of its agricultural production.[\[251\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:42-255) It is also the world's largest producer of [mohair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohair "Mohair") and [ostrich meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ostrich_meat "Ostrich meat").[\[252\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-256)

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_South_Africa "Science and technology in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Mark_Shuttleworth_NASA.jpg/500px-Mark_Shuttleworth_NASA.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mark_Shuttleworth_NASA.jpg) [Mark Shuttleworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Shuttleworth "Mark Shuttleworth") in space

Several important scientific and technological developments have originated in South Africa. The first human-to-human [heart transplant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_transplantation "Heart transplantation") was performed by cardiac surgeon [Christiaan Barnard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christiaan_Barnard "Christiaan Barnard") at [Groote Schuur Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groote_Schuur_Hospital "Groote Schuur Hospital") in December 1967; [Max Theiler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Theiler "Max Theiler") developed a [vaccine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_fever_vaccine "Yellow fever vaccine") against [yellow fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_fever "Yellow fever"), [Allan MacLeod Cormack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allan_MacLeod_Cormack "Allan MacLeod Cormack") pioneered X-ray computed tomography ( [CT scan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CT_scan "CT scan")); and [Aaron Klug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Klug "Aaron Klug") developed [crystallographic electron microscopy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electron_crystallography "Electron crystallography") techniques. Cormack and Klug received [Nobel Prizes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize "Nobel Prize") for their work. [Sydney Brenner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Brenner "Sydney Brenner") won in 2002, for his pioneering work in [molecular biology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molecular_biology "Molecular biology"). [Mark Shuttleworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Shuttleworth "Mark Shuttleworth") founded [Thawte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thawte "Thawte"), an early Internet security company pioneering digital certificates and online security.[\[253\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-257) South Africa was ranked 61st in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index "Global Innovation Index") in 2025, reflecting its continued scientific and technological capabilities.[\[254\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-258)[\[255\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-259)

South Africa has a burgeoning [astronomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astronomy "Astronomy") community and hosts the [Southern African Large Telescope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_African_Large_Telescope "Southern African Large Telescope"), the largest [optical telescope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optical_telescope "Optical telescope") in the [Southern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere "Southern Hemisphere"), and the [MeerKAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MeerKAT "MeerKAT") radio telescope, a precursor to the €1.5 billion [Square Kilometre Array](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Square_Kilometre_Array "Square Kilometre Array") project.[\[256\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-260) The [South African National Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Space_Agency "South African National Space Agency") (SANSA), established in 2010, oversees the country's space activities. South Africa launched its first locally developed satellite, [SUNSAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SUNSAT "SUNSAT"), in 1999 and had Africa's second-highest number of satellites in space, with 13 as of 2025, behind [Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt "Egypt") with 14.[\[257\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-261) South Africa is also the continent's leading satellite developer, with a high proportion of satellite components and systems developed locally.[\[258\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-262)[\[259\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-263)

South Africa has also made significant contributions to [military technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_technology "Military technology"). The country pioneered [mine-resistant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mine-resistant_ambush_protected_vehicle "Mine-resistant ambush protected vehicle") vehicle designs, including the [Buffel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffel "Buffel") and [Casspir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casspir "Casspir"), which established the use of V-shaped hulls to protect occupants from [landmines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_mine "Land mine") and later influenced modern MRAP vehicles.[\[260\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:2-264) South Africa also pioneered the operational use of [helmet-mounted sight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmet-mounted_display "Helmet-mounted display") technology on combat aircraft, successfully integrating a locally developed system with [air-to-air missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air-to-air_missile "Air-to-air missile") on [Mirage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Mirage_III "Dassault Mirage III") fighters in 1975.[\[261\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-265)[\[262\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-266)

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_South_Africa "Tourism in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Clifton_Beachs.jpg/500px-Clifton_Beachs.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Clifton_Beachs.jpg) [Clifton Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton,_Cape_Town "Clifton, Cape Town") in Cape Town is one of South Africa's most famous beaches

South Africa is a major international [tourist destination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_South_Africa "Tourism in South Africa"), known for its diverse landscapes, wildlife and cultural heritage. In 2024, it welcomed approximately 8.9 million international visitors through its various ports of entry. In 2025, _[The Daily Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Telegraph "The Daily Telegraph")_ ranked it the fourth-best country to visit worldwide and the highest-rated destination in Africa and the Indian Ocean region.[\[263\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-SAtour2-267) Tourism accounted for around 5.8% of South Africa's GDP in 2024.[\[264\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-268)

Tourist attractions include [Kruger National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kruger_National_Park "Kruger National Park"), [Table Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountain "Table Mountain"), the [Garden Route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garden_Route_National_Park "Garden Route National Park"), the [Drakensberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drakensberg "Drakensberg") and the beaches of the [Western Cape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape "Western Cape") and [KwaZulu-Natal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KwaZulu-Natal "KwaZulu-Natal"). [Safari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safari "Safari") tourism is a major attraction, supported by national parks and private game reserves.

Major cities also attract tourists. [Cape Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town "Cape Town") is known for Table Mountain, the [V&A Waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V&A_Waterfront "V&A Waterfront") and the nearby [Cape Winelands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boland,_Western_Cape "Boland, Western Cape"); [Johannesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johannesburg "Johannesburg") for attractions such as the [Apartheid Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid_Museum "Apartheid Museum"), [Constitution Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_Hill,_Johannesburg "Constitution Hill, Johannesburg") and [Gold Reef City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_Reef_City "Gold Reef City"); and [Durban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durban "Durban") for its beaches and [Golden Mile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Mile,_Durban "Golden Mile, Durban"). Wine tourism is also popular in the vineyards of [Stellenbosch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stellenbosch "Stellenbosch"), [Franschhoek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franschhoek "Franschhoek") and [Paarl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paarl "Paarl").

South Africa also has twelve [UNESCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO "UNESCO") [World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site "World Heritage Site"), including [Robben Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robben_Island "Robben Island"), the [Cradle of Humankind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cradle_of_Humankind "Cradle of Humankind"), [iSimangaliso Wetland Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISimangaliso_Wetland_Park "ISimangaliso Wetland Park") and the [Cape Floral Region Protected Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Floristic_Region "Cape Floristic Region").[\[265\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:4-269)

## Infrastructure

See also: [Housing in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_South_Africa "Housing in South Africa")

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_South_Africa "Transport in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Gautrain...%2C_O_R_Tambo_Intl_Airport_South_Africa.jpg/500px-Gautrain...%2C_O_R_Tambo_Intl_Airport_South_Africa.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gautrain...,_O_R_Tambo_Intl_Airport_South_Africa.jpg) A [Gautrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautrain "Gautrain") [higher-speed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher-speed_rail "Higher-speed rail") [express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Express_train "Express train") [commuter rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commuter_rail "Commuter rail") at [O. R. Tambo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O._R._Tambo_International_Airport "O. R. Tambo International Airport"), Johannesburg

South Africa has the largest and most extensive [transport infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport "Transport") in Africa, comprising an integrated network of [roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road "Road"), [railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport "Rail transport"), [airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airport "Airport"), [seaports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port "Port") and [pipelines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pipeline "Pipeline"). The country has approximately 750,000 kilometres (470,000mi) of roads, making it the largest [road network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_in_South_Africa "Roads in South Africa") in Africa and the [12th-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_road_network_size "List of countries by road network size") in the world. The [South African National Roads Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SANRAL "SANRAL") (SANRAL) manages the national road system,[\[266\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-270) including major highways such as the [N1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N1_(South_Africa) "N1 (South Africa)"), [N2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N2_(South_Africa) "N2 (South Africa)"), [N3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N3_(South_Africa) "N3 (South Africa)") and [N4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N4_(South_Africa) "N4 (South Africa)"), which connect the country's principal cities and form part of transcontinental routes such as the [Cape to Cairo Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo%E2%80%93Cape_Town_Highway "Cairo–Cape Town Highway").[\[267\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-271)

The [railway network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_South_Africa "Rail transport in South Africa") extends approximately 36,000 kilometres (23,700mi), making it the largest in Africa and the [seventh-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_rail_transport_network_size "List of countries by rail transport network size") in the world.[\[268\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-272) Freight services are operated primarily by [Transnet Freight Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnet_Freight_Rail "Transnet Freight Rail"),[\[269\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-273) while commuter rail services are provided by the [Passenger Rail Agency of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passenger_Rail_Agency_of_South_Africa "Passenger Rail Agency of South Africa") (PRASA). The [Gautrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gautrain "Gautrain") rapid rail system links Johannesburg and Pretoria, providing the country's only [higher-speed rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher-speed_rail "Higher-speed rail") service.[\[270\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-274)

South Africa has more than 1,500 [airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_South_Africa "List of airports in South Africa"),[\[271\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-275) among the highest numbers in the world and the greatest of any African country. [O.R. Tambo International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O._R._Tambo_International_Airport "O. R. Tambo International Airport") in Johannesburg is the largest and busiest airport in Africa,[\[272\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-276) serving as the country's principal international gateway, while [Cape Town International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Town_International_Airport "Cape Town International Airport"), [King Shaka International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Shaka_International_Airport "King Shaka International Airport") and [Chief Dawid Stuurman International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Dawid_Stuurman_International_Airport "Chief Dawid Stuurman International Airport") airports are other major hubs.[\[273\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-277) South Africa also has the continent's largest and most developed aviation market,[\[274\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-278) served by airlines including [South African Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_Airways "South African Airways"), [Airlink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airlink "Airlink"), [FlySafair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FlySafair "FlySafair"), [CemAir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CemAir "CemAir") and [LIFT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LIFT_(airline) "LIFT (airline)").[\[275\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-279)

South Africa's maritime sector is centred on the [commercial ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ports_and_harbours_in_South_Africa#List_of_ports_and_harbours_in_South_Africa "Ports and harbours in South Africa") of Durban, Cape Town, Gqeberha, Richards Bay, Saldanha Bay and East London.[\[276\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-280) The [Port of Durban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Durban "Port of Durban") is the busiest container port in sub-Saharan Africa and the fourth-largest in the Southern Hemisphere, while the [Port of Richards Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Richards_Bay "Port of Richards Bay") is one of the world's largest bulk export facilities.[\[277\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-281) Port infrastructure is managed by the [Transnet National Ports Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnet_National_Ports_Authority "Transnet National Ports Authority"), which plays a central role in supporting domestic logistics and international trade.[\[278\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-282)

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_South_Africa "Energy in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Koebergnps.jpg/500px-Koebergnps.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Koebergnps.jpg) The [Koeberg Nuclear Power Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koeberg_Nuclear_Power_Station "Koeberg Nuclear Power Station") in Cape Town, Africa's first and only commercial [nuclear power plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power_plant "Nuclear power plant")

South Africa has the largest and most advanced [energy sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_South_Africa "Energy in South Africa") in Africa and is the world's [22nd-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_electricity_production "List of countries by electricity production") producer of [electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity "Electricity"). The sector is dominated by the state-owned utility [Eskom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eskom "Eskom"), Africa's largest [power company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_utility "Electric utility"),[\[279\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-283) which generates approximately 90% of the country's electricity and is a major participant in the [Southern African Power Pool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_African_Power_Pool "Southern African Power Pool"), the regional electricity market. South Africa remains heavily reliant on [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_in_South_Africa "Coal in South Africa"),[\[280\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-284) producing more than 200 million tonnes annually, making it the world's [seventh-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_coal_production "List of countries by coal production") coal producer. Around 90% of the coal consumed on the African continent is mined in South Africa,[\[281\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-285) which is also one of the world's 15 largest [greenhouse gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions "Greenhouse gas emissions") emitters.[\[282\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-286)

The South African electricity grid has an installed capacity of around 60 GW.[\[283\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-287) In 2024, electricity generation was sourced from [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal "Coal") (approximately 80–83%), [renewable energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_South_Africa "Renewable energy in South Africa") from [wind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power "Wind power"), [solar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power "Solar power") and [hydroelectricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectricity "Hydroelectricity") (9–13%), [nuclear power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_power "Nuclear power") (around 4%) and other sources, including gas, diesel and pumped storage (4–6%).[\[284\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-288) Major generating facilities include the [Kusile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kusile_Power_Station "Kusile Power Station"), [Medupi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medupi_Power_Station "Medupi Power Station"), [Majuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majuba_Power_Station "Majuba Power Station") and [Kendal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kendal_Power_Station "Kendal Power Station") coal-fired power stations, while the [Koeberg Nuclear Power Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koeberg_Nuclear_Power_Station "Koeberg Nuclear Power Station") is Africa's first and only commercial nuclear power plant. Despite its substantial installed capacity, South Africa was embroiled in a prolonged [energy crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_energy_crisis "South African energy crisis") during the 2010s and early 2020s, resulting in recurring [load-shedding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_blackout "Rolling blackout").[\[285\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-289)

Following the stabilisation of the electricity system in 2024,[\[286\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-290) government policy now focuses on diversifying the electricity mix through the [Integrated Resource Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_Resource_Plan "Integrated Resource Plan") 2025,[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:8-291) which targets 105 GW of new generation capacity by 2039 and represents an investment of approximately R2.2 trillion (US$120 billion), making it the single largest investment programme in South Africa's post-apartheid history.[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:9-292) The plan invites [independent power producers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_power_producer "Independent power producer") to contribute to the expansion, with the projected 2039 generation mix consisting of coal (27%), wind power (24%), grid-connected solar power (18%), rooftop solar (10%), nuclear power (5%), hydroelectricity (4%), diesel (4.5%) and other sources (7.5%), representing a substantial shift away from coal.[\[287\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:8-291)[\[288\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:9-292)

### Water supply and sanitation

Main article: [Water supply and sanitation in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_South_Africa "Water supply and sanitation in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Dam_wall_of_the_Blyde_river_dam-_Hoedspruit_Limpopo_province_South_Africa_28th_March_2008_%2839536420642%29.jpg/500px-Dam_wall_of_the_Blyde_river_dam-_Hoedspruit_Limpopo_province_South_Africa_28th_March_2008_%2839536420642%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Dam_wall_of_the_Blyde_river_dam-_Hoedspruit_Limpopo_province_South_Africa_28th_March_2008_(39536420642).jpg) The [Blyde River Dam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blyderivierpoort_Dam "Blyderivierpoort Dam") in Mpumalanga

Two distinctive features of the South African water sector are the policy of free basic water and the existence of [water boards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_Board_(South_Africa) "Water Board (South Africa)"), which are bulk water supply agencies that operate pipelines and sell water from reservoirs to municipalities. These features have led to significant problems concerning the financial sustainability of service providers, leading to a lack of attention to maintenance. Following the end of apartheid, the country had made improvements in the levels of access to water as those with access increased from 66% to 79% from 1990 to 2010.[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-JMP-293) Sanitation access increased from 71% to 79% during the same period.[\[289\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-JMP-293) However, water supply and sanitation has come under increasing pressure in recent years despite a commitment made by the government to improve service standards and provide investment subsidies to the water industry.[\[290\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-294)

The eastern parts of South Africa suffer from periodic droughts linked to the [El Niño](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Ni%C3%B1o "El Niño") weather phenomenon.[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-whyC-295) In early 2018, Cape Town, which has different weather patterns to the rest of the country,[\[291\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-whyC-295) faced a water crisis as the city's water supply was predicted to run dry before the end of June. Water-saving measures were in effect that required each citizen to use less than 50 litres (13USgal) per day.[\[292\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-296) Cape Town rejected an offer from Israel to help it build [desalination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desalination "Desalination") plants.[\[293\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-297)[\[294\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-298)[\[295\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-299)[\[296\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-300)

### Telecommunications

Main articles: [Telecommunications in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_South_Africa "Telecommunications in South Africa"), [Internet in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_in_South_Africa "Internet in South Africa"), and [Internet censorship in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_censorship_in_South_Africa "Internet censorship in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Bar_Chart_Showing_South_African_Internet_Usage_by_Year.png/500px-Bar_Chart_Showing_South_African_Internet_Usage_by_Year.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bar_Chart_Showing_South_African_Internet_Usage_by_Year.png) Growth in internet usage among the South African population, by year

South Africa has the most advanced [telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications "Telecommunications") sector in Africa, regulated by the [Independent Communications Authority of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Communications_Authority_of_South_Africa "Independent Communications Authority of South Africa") (ICASA).[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:3-301) The country serves as a key regional hub for digital connectivity, supported by several major [undersea cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_cable "Submarine cable") systems such as [WACS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WACS_(cable_system) "WACS (cable system)"), [SAT-3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SAT-3/WASC "SAT-3/WASC"), [Seacom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEACOM_(African_cable_system) "SEACOM (African cable system)"), and [2Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2Africa "2Africa"), which link South Africa to global networks.[\[298\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-302) Mobile penetration is among the highest on the continent, and [internet access](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_access "Internet access") continues to expand rapidly across urban and rural areas.[\[297\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-:3-301)

The [mobile communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cellular_network "Cellular network") market is led by major South African operators including [Vodacom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodacom "Vodacom"), [MTN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTN_Group "MTN Group"), [Telkom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telkom_(South_Africa) "Telkom (South Africa)"), [Cell C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cell_C "Cell C"), and [Rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rain_(telecommunications) "Rain (telecommunications)"). MTN, in particular, stands out as Africa's largest mobile network operator and one of the top ten globally, with nearly 290 million users as of 2022.[\[299\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-303) South Africa offers a full range of network technologies from [2G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2G "2G") to [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G "5G"), with MTN, Vodacom, and Rain being the first to launch 5G services on the entire African continent.[\[300\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-304)[\[301\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-305) While [fixed-line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fixed-line "Fixed-line") connections have declined, [fibre-optic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiber-optic_communication "Fiber-optic communication") [broadband](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadband "Broadband") is growing quickly through providers such as [Openserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Openserve "Openserve"), [Vumatel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vumatel "Vumatel"), [Frogfoot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frogfoot_(South_Africa) "Frogfoot (South Africa)"), [Octotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octotel "Octotel"), and [MetroFibre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_broadband_providers_in_South_Africa "List of broadband providers in South Africa").[\[302\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-306) [Satellite internet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satellite_Internet_access "Satellite Internet access") remains available for remote regions, though [Starlink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starlink "Starlink") has yet to launch locally due to regulatory restrictions.[\[303\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-307)

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_South_Africa "Demographics of South Africa") and [List of municipalities in South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_South_Africa "List of municipalities in South Africa")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/South_Africa_2011_population_density_map_%28hex_cells%29.svg/500px-South_Africa_2011_population_density_map_%28hex_cells%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:South_Africa_2011_population_density_map_(hex_cells).svg) Map of population density in South Africa

- <1 /km2

- 1–3 /km2

- 3–10 /km2

- 10–30 /km2

- 30–100 /km2

- 100–300 /km2

- 300–1000 /km2

- 1000–3000 /km2

- >3000 /km2


South Africa is a nation of about 62 million (as of 2022) people of diverse origins, cultures, languages, and religions.[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-Census-2022-11) The last [census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_National_Census_of_2022 "South African National Census of 2022") was held in 2022, with estimates produced on an annual basis. According to the _[United Nations World Population Prospects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_World_Population_Prospects "United Nations World Population Prospects")_, South Africa's total population was 55.3 million in 2015, compared to only 13.6 million in 1950.[\[304\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-308) South Africa is home to an estimated fivemillion [illegal immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_immigration "Illegal immigration"), including some threemillion Zimbabweans.[\[305\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-309)[\[306\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-310)[\[307\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-311) A series of [anti-immigrant riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xenophobia_in_South_Africa "Xenophobia in South Africa") occurred beginning in May 2008.[\[308\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-bbc-312)[\[309\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-313)

[Statistics South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statistics_South_Africa "Statistics South Africa") asks people to describe themselves in the census in terms of five racial population groups.[\[310\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-314) The 2022 census figures for these groups were: [Black African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantu_peoples_of_South_Africa "Bantu peoples of South Africa") at 81%, [Coloured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_Coloureds "Cape Coloureds") at 8.2%, [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_South_Africans "White South Africans") at 7.3%, [Indian or Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_South_Africans "Indian South Africans") at 2.7%, and Other/Unspecified at 0.5%.[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-Census-2022-11) The first census in 1911 showed that whites made up 22% of the population; this had declined to 16% by 1980.[\[311\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-315)

South Africa hosts a sizeable refugee and asylum seeker population. According to the _World Refugee Survey 2008_, published by the [U.S. Committee for Refugees and Immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Committee_for_Refugees_and_Immigrants "U.S. Committee for Refugees and Immigrants"), this population numbered approximately 144,700 in 2007.[\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-World_Refugee_Survey_2008-316) Groups of refugees and asylum seekers numbering over 10,000 included people from Zimbabwe (48,400), the DRC (24,800), and Somalia (12,900).[\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-World_Refugee_Survey_2008-316) These populations mainly lived in Johannesburg, Pretoria, [Durban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durban "Durban"), Cape Town, and [Port Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Elizabeth "Port Elizabeth").[\[312\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-World_Refugee_Survey_2008-316)

One online database[\[313\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa#cite_note-317) lists South Africa having more than 12,600 cities and towns.
